**PART A — Parmar's SSC Fatman (2nd Ed., 2026): "ADVENT OF EUROPEANS" (book pp.155–158, PDF 171–174)**

Parmar's Modern History section opens with a chapter-map page (PDF 170, unpaginated) listing this chapter's sub-headings as: Portuguese · Dutch · English · Expansion of Britishers · Carnatic Wars · In Bengal · In Mysore · In Punjab · Afghans · Burma. Chapter closes with a "■■■" end marker at the bottom of book p.158 (confirmed by direct read).

Note: Parmar's chapter title is "Advent of Europeans" but, unlike Lucent, it folds the whole British-expansion narrative (Bengal, Mysore, Subsidiary Alliance, Punjab, Afghans, Burma, Nepal) into this one chapter. Lucent splits the same ground into ch.15 (Advent) and ch.16 (Expansion of British Power).

**\[book p.155 / PDF 171\]**

**Order of Advent of Europeans in India**

1. Portuguese (1498)
2. Dutch (1602)
3. English (1608)
4. Danes (1616)
5. French (1668)

**PORTUGUESE**

_Arrival of Portuguese_

- The Portuguese came to India for various economic and religious reasons. They were the _first to introduce tobacco and cashew nuts_ in India. They were the first to arrive and the last to leave. Goa was liberated in 1961.

_Vasco da Gama (1498)_

- Vasco da Gama was the first to discover the sea route to India via the _Cape of Good Hope_.
- He was a Portuguese explorer who came to _Calicut_ in 1498 during the rule of _King Zamorin_ (then Hindu ruler).
- He reached Calicut aboard the Portuguese ship _São Gabriel_, guided by an _Arab navigator_ named _Ahmad ibn Majid (Ibn Majid)_.

\[Box\] _Treaty of Tordesillas, signed on 7th June 1494 was a significant agreement between Spain and Portugal. It aimed to resolve conflicts over newly discovered lands by dividing the world into two spheres of influence. The treaty established a demarcation line, with lands to the east belonging to Portugal and lands to the west to Spain._

_Francisco de Almeida (1505)_

- He was the _first Portuguese Viceroy of India_.
- He brought _"Blue Water Policy"_ (Cartaz System) which was a trade licensing system that allowed the Portuguese to control trade in _Indian Ocean_.

_Afonso de Albuquerque (1509)_

- He was the _second Portuguese Governor of India_. He is considered as the real founder of Portuguese power.
- He captured Goa in 1510 (from Adil Shah of Bijapur) and abolished _Sati_.
- He received permission to build a fort at _Bhatkal_ from Krishnadevaraya.

_Nuno da Cunha_

- He was a Portuguese Governor who moved capital from _Cochin_ to _Goa_ in 1530.

\[Box\] _Bahadur Shah, ruler of Gujarat surrendered Diu to Portuguese in 1535._

**DUTCH**

_Arrival of Dutch_

- Dutch refers to _"people from Netherlands"_.
- Dutch East India Company was formed in _1602_.
- They set up their first factory in _Masulipatnam_ (Machilipatnam) in Andhra Pradesh in _1605_.

**ENGLISH**

_Arrival of English_

- _1599:_ East India Company came into existence.
- _1600:_ East India Company received a _Royal Charter_ from _Queen Elizabeth I_ to trade in India.
- _1608: William Hawkins_ was sent by _King James I_ of England and arrived at the court of Jahangir.
- _1611:_ First factory at Masulipatnam (temporary).
- _1613:_ First factory at Surat (permanent).
- _1615: Sir Thomas Roe_ visited the court of Jahangir (arriving on the English ship _Hector_) to obtain official trading rights for the English East India Company.
- _1620:_ Danes established their first factory at _Tranquebar_ (Tamil Nadu).
- _1668:_ French established their first factory at _Surat_.

**EXPANSION OF BRITISHERS**

- _Battle of Swally/Suvali (1612):_ Fought in Surat. The British defeated the Portuguese.
- _Battle of Chinsurah/Bedara/Hooghly (1759):_ Fought on the banks of the Hooghly River. The British defeated the Dutch.

\[Box\] _Battle of Colachel in 1741 between Marthanda Varma (King of Travancore) and Dutch East India Company. The Dutch were defeated._

**CARNATIC WARS**

- The series of Carnatic Wars started between British and French East India Companies.
- A total of 3 Carnatic Wars were fought between _1744_ and _1763_.
- _Dupleix_ led the French forces in the first two Carnatic Wars.

_1st Carnatic War (1744-1748)_

- Extension of the Austrian War of Succession.
- _Battle of Adyar (1746)_ is also called the Battle of St. Thome.
- It ended with _Treaty of Aix-la-Chapelle_.
- Won by: _French East India Company_.

**\[book p.156 / PDF 172\]**

_2nd Carnatic War (1749-1754)_

- It started with _Battle of Ambur (1749)_ was won by the French forces led by Joseph François Dupleix, supporting Chanda Sahib.
- It ended with _Treaty of Pondicherry_.
- Note: _Dupleix_ led French forces in the first two wars.

_3rd Carnatic War (1756-1763)_

- _Battle of Wandiwash (1760)_ was a part of 3rd Carnatic War.
- This war was an extension of Anglo-French _War of 7 years_ which was caused by _Austrian War of Succession_.
- It ended with _Treaty of Paris_.
- British forces led by _Sir Eyre Coote_ defeated the French forces led by _Comte de Lally_.
- Result: French were limited to their enclaves (Pondicherry, etc.) and no further expansion was allowed.

**IN BENGAL**

- _1717-1727: Murshid Quli Khan_ served as the _first Nawab of Bengal_.
- In 1717, Mughal Emperor _Farrukhsiyar_ issued a _Golden Farman_ (Magna Carta of Trade) that allowed the British East India Company to trade freely in _Bengal_ for a yearly payment of 3,000 Rupees. The farman also granted the company the right to issue _Dastaks_, or trade permits, to move goods.

\[Box\] _Nawabs of Bengal:_ _Murshid Quli Khan → Shuja-ud-Din Muhammad Khan → Alivardi Khan (died in: 1756) → Siraj-ud-Daulah_

_Siraj-ud-Daulah_

- He became the _Nawab_ at the age of 23 years after the death of _Alivardi Khan_ in 1756.
- He was the _last self-governing_ Nawab of Bengal.
- He was not happy with the Trade privileges enjoyed by Britishers in Bengal.
- Siraj-ud-Daulah, captured the _Fort William_ after the British refused to stop fortifying it. The _Black Hole Tragedy_ was a night of captivity for British soldiers and other prisoners in a small, airless room in Fort William, Calcutta in _1756_. This incident instigated _Robert Clive_, who decided to take revenge, which led to the Battle of Plassey.
- \[Image caption, meme-style picture: Robert Clive / Mir Jafar / Siraj-ud-Daulah — "GADDARI KARWE !!"\]

\[Box\] _Treaty of Alinagar (1757)_ — _It was signed between Siraj-ud-Daulah and the Britishers which became the cause of Battle of Plassey._

_Battle of Plassey (1757)_

- It was fought between the East India Company force led by _Robert Clive_ and _Siraj-ud-Daulah_ (Nawab of Bengal).
- _Mir Jafar_, the military commander of Siraj-ud-Daulah joined hands with the Britishers.
- This battle confirmed the formal beginning of British Empire in India.

_Outcomes of Battle of Plassey_

- Siraj-Ud-Daulah was killed (forces led by Mir Madan).
- Mir Jafar was made the new puppet Nawab of Bengal.
- Then Mughal Emperor: _Alamgir II_.
- Subsequently, _Mir Qasim_ (son-in-law of Mir Jafar) came to the throne. He shifted the capital from _Murshidabad_ to _Munger_ (he also ended Dastak issued by Farrukhsiyar).
- Combined forces _Shuja-ud-daulah_ (Nawab of Awadh), _Mir Qasim_ and _Shah Alam II_ (Mughal Emperor) led to _Battle of Buxar (1764)_ against the Britishers.

\[Box\] _1st Nawab of Awadh: Saadat Khan Burhan-ul-Mulk._ / _The fourth Nawab of Awadh → Nawab Asaf-ud-Daula, built Bara Imambara in Lucknow._

_Battle of Buxar (1764)_

- Britishers won this battle led by _Hector Munro_.
- _Treaty of Allahabad_ was signed in 1765 between _Shuja-ud-Daula_ (Nawab of Awadh), Mughal Emperor _Shah Alam II_ and was negotiated by _Robert Clive_ on behalf of the East India Company.

Under this, two separate treaty were signed.

_First Treaty with Nawab of Awadh:_

- _Allahabad_ and _Kara_ were handed over to Mughal Emperor Shah Alam II.
- Nawab paid Rs 50 lakh to the British as war indemnity.
- \[Image caption: "Hector Munro / Robert Clive / Shah Alam II — Scenes From Treaty of Allahabad"\]

_Second Treaty with Shah Alam II:_

- Emperor Shah Alam II was placed under British protection at Allahabad.
- _Diwani_ (revenue rights) of _Bengal, Bihar,_ and _Orissa_ were given to the East India Company, in return of an annual payment of Rs 26 lakh.
- The Company was to receive Rs 53 lakh from the Emperor for performing nizamat (military and judicial) functions in these provinces. This control over _Diwani_ and _Nizamat_ Functions by East India Company is called as _Dual Government in Bengal_.
- Mir Jafar was made the Nawab again.
- Allahabad Treaty ran for _7 years_ and was ended by _Warren Hastings_ in 1772.

**\[book p.157 / PDF 173\]**

**IN MYSORE**

- In Mysore, the _Wodeyar Dynasty_ was ruling. _Chikka Devaraja Wodeyar_ strengthened and consolidated the Mysore state.
- _Hyder Ali_ began his career as a soldier under the Wodeyars and gradually rose to power, eventually becoming the de facto ruler of Mysore, while the Wodeyars continued as nominal rulers.

_Anglo-Mysore Wars_

_1st Anglo-Mysore War (1767-1769)_

- The East India Company, Marathas, and the Nizam of Hyderabad were on one side, while Haider Ali was on the other.
- It ended with the _Treaty of Madras (1769)_, in which the British assured assistance to Haider Ali in the event of an attack by the Marathas or the Nizam.
- _Won by: Mysore._

_2nd Anglo-Mysore War (1780-1784)_

- Cause: Violation of the Madras Treaty by the British.
- Hyder Ali was defeated in the _battle of Porto Novo (1781)_ by the British forces led by Sir Eyre Coote.
- Tipu Sultan sat on throne after the death of his father Hyder Ali due to cancer in the middle of the war.
- There was no winner in this war. It ended with _Treaty of Mangalore._

_3rd Anglo-Mysore War (1790-1792)_

- The British were led by _Lord Cornwallis._
- It ended with _Treaty of Seringapatnam._
- _Tipu Sultan_ was defeated in this war (Capital: Seringapatam).
- Under this treaty, a heavy war indemnity of ₹3 crore was imposed on Tipu Sultan.
- Additionally, he had to cede half of his kingdom to the British, Marathas, and the Nizam, and two of his sons were taken as hostages.

_4th Anglo-Mysore War (1798-1799)_

- Tipu established the Jacobin Club and declared himself "Citizen Tipu".
- He planted the _"Tree of Liberty"_ in his capital, Seringapatam.
- He established contact with Napoleon Bonaparte and invited him to come to India to launch a joint struggle against the British.
- Due to growing proximity of Tipu Sultan with French, Britishers waged another war with him.
- _Tipu Sultan_ was killed in the Battle.
- Result: The independent power of Mysore came to an end.
- The Mysore state was handed back to the Wodeyar dynasty (under British suzerainty).
- British power was fully established in South India.

**Subsidiary Alliance**

- Subsidiary alliance was introduced by _Lord Wellesley_ in _1798_, though it was a brain-child of French Governor General _Dupleix._
- Under the subsidiary alliance, Indian rulers had to pay subsidy for the British Garrison and British Army deployed in their territory. They had to seek permission from British EIC to partner with any other European for any service, and also for the negotiations with other Indian rulers.
- _Adopted by:_ Hyderabad (first State to sign in 1798); Mysore (in 1799); Tanjore (in 1799); Awadh (in 1801) and Peshwa (in 1802).

**IN PUNJAB**

- _Maharaja Ranjit Singh_ from _Sukerchakia Misl_ (one of the 12 Sikh Misls) signed _Treaty of Amritsar_ in _1809_ with British and declared _Sutlej river_ as boundary between the Sikh Kingdom and the British Empire.
- After the death of Maharaja Ranjit Singh (1839), political instability spread in Punjab.
- _Maharaja Duleep Singh_ ascended the throne at a very young age.

_Anglo-Sikh Wars_

_1st Anglo-Sikh War (1845–1846)_

- _Cause:_ The British violated the terms of the Treaty of Amritsar (1809).
- _Lord Hardinge_ was the Governor-General of India at this time.
- _Treaty of Lahore (1846)_ (Lahore was the capital) and _Treaty of Bhairowal (1846)_ were signed between _Maharaja Duleep Singh_ and British.
- _Treaty of Amritsar (1846):_ In this treaty, the British handed over the Kashmir region to Raja Gulab Singh. In exchange, Raja Gulab Singh paid ₹75 lakh.
- It resulted in the defeat and partial subjugation of the Sikh Empire and cession of Jammu & Kashmir as a separate princely state under British suzerainty.
- \[Box\] _Battle of Aliwal (1846)_ — It was a part of 1st Anglo-Sikh war. The British under Sir Harry Smith defeated the Sikh forces.

_2nd Anglo-Sikh War (1848)_

- _Mulraj_, the Governor of Multan, was dissatisfied with British control.
- In 1848, Mulraj killed two British officers of the East India Company. This incident sparked the Sikh rebellion.
- _Maharaja Duleep Singh_ was captured and sent to Britain and _Kohinoor_ diamond was stolen. _Lord Dalhousie_ was then _Governor-General of India._

**\[book p.158 / PDF 174\]**

**AFGHANS**

- _Background:_ In the 19th century, the Tsarist Empire (Russia) was expanding rapidly towards Central Asia. The British feared that Russia might attack India, which was the most important colony of the British Empire.
- _Buffer State Policy:_ It was considered necessary to make Afghanistan a buffer state to safeguard British India from external threats (especially Russia).
- _Buffer State:_ A state located between two powerful countries to prevent direct conflict between them.
- _Ring Fence Policy: Warren Hastings_ adopted this policy in 1765.
  - _Objective:_ To create a protective ring of friendly/allied states around Company territories to safeguard them from external threats.
  - _Nature:_ Defensive policy (focused on protection rather than expansion).
- _Forward Policy:_ Associated with _Lord Auckland._
  - It was believed that if Russia advanced towards India, it was better to intervene in Afghanistan beforehand.
  - This policy led to British intervention in Afghanistan, resulting in the First Anglo-Afghan War.

_Tripartite Treaty (1838):_

- The Tripartite Treaty was signed in 1838 under the Forward Policy of Lord Auckland.
- This treaty was between three parties: 1. British Government 2. Shuja Shah Durrani (Afghan Ruler) 3. Ranjit Singh (Sikh Ruler)
- _Objective:_ To remove Dost Mohammad Khan and install Shuja Shah on the throne of Afghanistan. To bring Afghanistan under British influence.

_1st Anglo-Afghan War (1839-1842)_

- Fought during the tenure of _Lord Auckland_ (Governor-General of India).
- Initially, the British succeeded in removing _Dost Mohammad Khan_ and placed _Shah Shuja Durrani_ on the throne of Afghanistan.
- However, this success was short-lived and a rebellion led to the collapse of British control.
- Result: The British were defeated and forced to retreat from Kabul (1842); the retreat was nearly annihilated, marking one of the worst defeats in British Indian history.
- Later, _John Lawrence_ (Governor-General) adopted the _Policy of Masterly Inactivity_ towards Afghanistan.

_2nd Anglo-Afghan War (1878-1880)_

- _Treaty of Gandamak_ was signed between the British Government and Afghan ruler _Yakub Khan._
- Afghanistan's foreign policy went under British control.
- Some frontier areas were given to British India
- Lord Lytton implemented the "Proud Reserve Policy" of Britain.
- The _Durand Line_ was drawn in 1893 to define the boundary between Afghanistan and British India. This laid the foundation for today's Afghanistan-Pakistan border.
- _Between:_ British India and Afghan Ruler _Amir Abdur Rahman Khan._
- _Drawn by:_ Sir Mortimer Durand.

_3rd Anglo-Afghan War (1919)_

- Occurred during the viceroyalty of _Lord Chelmsford_; led by _Amanullah Khan_, it ended with the _Treaty of Rawalpindi_, granting Afghanistan control over its foreign affairs.

**BURMA (MYANMAR)**

_1st Anglo Burma War (1824–1826)_

- It ended with the Treaty of Yandaboo (1826).

_2nd Anglo Burma War (1852-1853)_

- \[no further detail printed\]

**OTHER WARS & EVENTS**

_Battle of Saragarhi (1897):_

- The Battle of Saragarhi occurred during a tribal uprising on the North-West Frontier.
- It took place in present-day Khyber Pakhtunkhwa (Pakistan).
- It was associated with the Tirah Campaign (1897-98).
- 21 soldiers of the 36th Sikh Regiment fought against thousands of tribal warriors.

_Anglo-Nepal War (1814–1816):_

- The _Treaty of Sugauli_ was signed after this war in 1816.
- Through this treaty, the border between British India and Nepal was determined, and Nepal had to cede some territories.

**Part A — flags for this chapter (⚠ = doubt about the printed statement; source text transcribed as printed, not corrected)**

- ⚠ **Ahmad ibn Majid as Vasco da Gama's pilot (p.155)** — printed as fact; this is a traditional but historically disputed claim. Lucent (ch.15) says nothing about a pilot.
- ⚠ **"Almeida = first Portuguese Viceroy" (Parmar p.155) vs "first governor" (Lucent ch.15)** — same person, different title; not a factual conflict, just wording.
- ⚠ **Order-list dates vs body dates (p.155)**: list gives English (1608), French (1668), Danes (1616); Lucent's table gives company-founding dates 1600 (English), 1664 (French), 1616 (Danish). Parmar's own body text reconciles it (1600 charter; 1608 Hawkins; 1668 first French factory at Surat) — the list dates are arrival/first-contact dates, not founding dates. Not a true conflict.
- ⚠ **Parmar "Battle of Adyar (1746) = Battle of St. Thome"** — matches Lucent's "St. Thome battle" for the First Carnatic War. Consistent.
- ⚠ **Nawabs of Bengal box (p.156)** omits Sarfaraz Khan (between Shuja-ud-Din and Alivardi Khan). Printed chain only as given.
- ⚠ **"Company was to receive Rs 53 lakh from the Emperor" (p.156)** — as printed. Not cross-checked against Lucent (its ch.16 Bengal section is the place to compare — see Part B).
- ⚠ **"Mir Jafar was made the Nawab again" listed under the 1765 Allahabad treaties (p.156)** — as printed. Chronology looks off (Mir Jafar was restored in 1763 and died in early 1765); check against Lucent's Bengal section (Part B) before relying on it.
- ⚠ **"Ring Fence Policy: Warren Hastings adopted this policy in 1765" (p.158)** — as printed. Warren Hastings' governorship began 1772; 1765 was Clive's second governorship. Not found in Lucent's ch.16 or in its closing Governor-General section (checked).
- ⚠ **"John Lawrence (Governor-General)" (p.158)** — as printed; by 1864 the office was styled Viceroy. Parmar's own Governor-General/Viceroy chapter (book pp.185–186) does not list John Lawrence at all; Lucent's Governor-General section lists "Sir John Lawrence (1864-69)" under "Governor Generals and Viceroys (1858-1947)" (see governors_general_and_viceroys_raw_extraction.md).
- ⚠ **Subsidiary Alliance as "brain-child of French Governor General Dupleix" (p.157)** — as printed (Dupleix was French governor at Pondicherry, not "Governor General"). Lucent's Subsidiary-Alliance material is in ch.16 (Part B) and its Governor-General section (Wellesley: "Started Subsidiary Alliance system … Nizam Ali (Nizam of Hyderabad) was the first Indian native ruler to accept … (1798)"); neither mentions Dupleix in that context.
- ⚠ **2nd Anglo-Burma War (1852-1853)** — heading only, no content in Parmar; Lucent's Governor-General section has "2nd Anglo-Burmese War (1852) and annexation of Lower Burma or Pegu" under Dalhousie (see governors_general_and_viceroys_raw_extraction.md); Part B (ch.16) has none.

**\[book p.68 / PDF 78\]**

**Modern India**

**16\. Expansion of British Power** _(In the context of Bengal, Mysore, Punjab etc.)_

**Bengal**

- _Murshild Quli Khan (1717-27)_: In 1717, Murshid Quli Khan was appointed as Bengal's Subedar i.e. governor by Mughal emperor Farrukh Siyar. Grant of the Governorship of Orissa also to him by Farrukh Siyar in 1719. He transferred the capital of Bengal from Dacca to _Murshidabad_.
- _Shujauddin (1727-39)_: He was the son-in-law of Murshid Quli Khan. He was granted the Governorship of Bihar by Mughal emperor Muhammad Shah 'Rangeela' in 1733.
- _Sarfaraj Khan (1739-40)_: He was the son of Shujauddin and was murdered by Alivardi Khan, the Deputy Governor of Bihar, in 1740.
- _Alivardi Khan (1740-56)_: Legalised his usurpation by receiving a _farman_ from Mughal emperor Muhammad Shah 'Rangeela' after paying him Rs. 2 Crores. He prevented the English and the French from fortifying their factories at Calcutta and Chandranagore respectively.

**Sirajuddaula : 1756-57**

- Alivardi Khan was succeeded by his grandson Sirajuddaula.
- Sirajuddaula seized the English factory at Kasimbazar. On 20th June, 1756, Fort William surrendered but Robert Clive recovered Calcutta.
- On 9th Feb. 1757, _Treaty of Alinagar_ was signed, where by Siraj conceded practically all the demands. British then captured Chandranagore, the French settlement, on March 1757.
- The _Battle of Plassey_ was fought on 23 June, 1757. Owing to a conspiracy, the Nawab was defeated.
- The following betrayed the Nawab:
  - _Mir Jafar_: Mir Bakshi
  - _Manikchand_: Officer in charge of Calcutta
  - _Amichand_: Rich Sikh merchant
  - _Jagat Seth_: Biggest banker of Bengal
  - _Khadim Khan_: Commanded a large number of Nawab's troops.

**Mir Jafar : 1757-60** The company was granted undisputed right to free trade in Bengal, Bihar and Orissa. It received the zamindari of 24 Parganas. Mir Jafar, however, fell into arrears and was forced to abdicate in favour of his son-in-law Mir Qasim.

**Mir Qasim : 1760-64**

- Mir Qasim ceded Burdwan, Midnapore and Chittagong. He shifted his capital from Murshidabad to _Munger_.
- Mir Qasim soon revolted as he was angry with the British for misusing the _dastak_ (free duty passes). However, having been defeated by the British, he fled to Awadh, where he formed a confederacy with Awadh ruler Shujauddaula and Mughal emperor Shah Alam II.
- The _Battle of Buxar (1764)_: Mir Qasim, Shujauddaula and Shah Alam II were defeated by Munro.
- Mir Jafar was again placed on the throne.
- Successors of Mir Qasim : Mir Jafar (1764-65), Nazmuddaulah (1765-66), Saifuddaula (1766-70), Mubaraquddaula (1770-72).
- On Mir Jafar's death, his son Nazmuddaula was placed on the throne and signed a treaty on 20th Feb., 1765 by which the Nawab was to disband most of his army and to administer Bengal through a Deputy Subedar nominated by the Company.
- Clive concluded two separate _treaties of Allahabad_ with Shah Alam II (12 Aug., 1765) and Shujauddaula (16 Aug., 1765).

**\[book p.69 / PDF 79\]**

**Dual Government of Bengal : 1765-72**

- Dual Government of Bengal started in 1765.
- The Company acquired both _Diwani_ and _Nizamat_ rights from Nazmuddaula, the Nawab of Bengal. But the company did not take over direct administration and revenue collection.
- Warren Hastings ended the dual system of government in 1772.

**Mysore**

_Haidar Ali : 1761-82_

- Haidar Ali began his career as a soldier in the service of the Mysore state, later he became the faujdar of _Dindigul_. He established a modern arsenal in Dindigul with the help of the French.
- In 1761, he overthrew the _Nanjarajar_ (the powerful Prime Minister of Wodeyar king Krishnaraja-I) and usurped power, though he continued to recognise Krishnaraja I as the lawful ruler.
- _First Anglo-Mysore war (1766-69)_: Haider Ali defeated the British. The _Treaty of Madras (1769)_ signed.
- _Second Anglo-Mysore war (1780-84)_: Warren Hastings attacked French port Mahe, which was in Haidar Ali's territory.
- Haidar Ali led a joint front with Nizam and Maratha and captured Arcot _(Capital of Carnatic state)_.
- In 1781, Haidar Ali was defeated at Porto Novo by Eyrecoot.
- He died during the Second Anglo-Mysore war.

_Tipu Sultan 1782-99_

- Haidar Ali was succeeded by his son Tipu Sultan in 1782.
- He continued the Second Anglo-Mysore war till 1784.
- The _Treaty of Mangalore (1784)_ was signed by Tipu Sultan which ended the Second Anglo-Mysore war.
- _Third Anglo-Mysore war (1790-92)_: Maratha and Nizam aided the British, Cornwallis captured Bangalore. By the _Treaty of Seringapatnam (1792)_, Tipu ceded half of his territory.
- _Fourth Anglo-Mysore war (1799)_: Lord Wellesly attacked and Tipu Sultan died.
- Tipu was the only Indian ruler who understood the importance of economic strength as the foundation of military strength.
- Tipu established embassies to France, Turkey, Iran and Pegu to develop foreign trade.
- Tipu planted a 'tree of liberty' at his capital Seringapatnam and became a member of the Jacobin Club.

**Punjab**

- Guru Gobind Singh, the 10th and the last Guru of the Sikhs, transformed the religious sect into a military brotherhood.
- In the confusion and disorder that followed the invasion of Nadir Shah and Ahmad Shah Abdali, the sikhs increased their military strength and became a strong power.
- _Maharaja Ranjit Singh (1792-1839)_: He was the greatest Indian ruler of his time and founder of the Sikh rule in the Punjab. Born in 1780 at Gujranwala, he occupied Lahore in 1799 and made it his capital. He conquered Amritsar in 1802, occupied Ludhiana and after incessant wars, annexed Kangra, Attock, Multan, Kashmir, Hazara, Bannu, Derajat and Peshawar. He died in 1839.
- Successors of Ranjit Singh : _Kharak Singh_ (1839-40), _Naunihal Singh_ (1840), _Sher Singh_ (1841-43), _Dalip Singh_ (1843-49).
- The Sikh power was broken by the British after the death of Ranjit Singh.
- _First Anglo-Sikh war (1845-46)_: Sikhs were defeated in all the four battles at Mudki, Ferozshah, Aliwal and Sobraon. The _Treaty of Lahore (1846)_ ended the war. Sir Henry Lawerence became the first resident.
- _Second Anglo-Sikh war (1848-49)_: Dalhousie annexed Punjab. Sir John Lawerence became the first chief commissioner of Punjab.

**PART C — Cross-references and Part A vs Part B comparison (no test cross-referencing — books only)**

**C1. Cross-reference to Medieval-side extraction (not re-extracted)**

- Lucent ch.15 "The Advent of the Europeans" (Portuguese, Dutch, English, French, Danish companies table; Anglo-French/Carnatic Wars) → **claude/medieval-history-raw/advent_of_europeans_raw_extraction.md Part A** (already extracted, single-source Lucent). Nothing from it is repeated above.
- **Correction to that file's premise**: its banner and the Medieval build notes §5c state that "Parmar has **zero equivalent content** anywhere" for the Advent of Europeans. That was true only for Parmar's _Medieval_ chapters. **Parmar's Modern History section opens with exactly this topic** ("Advent of Europeans", book pp.155–158, PDF 171–174 — Part A above). So the Lucent-only file and Part A here are the two halves of the two-book coverage. The medieval file should now be read together with Part A above; its "single-source, uncross-checked" warning is no longer accurate. (Medieval files are otherwise untouched by this task.)
- Overlap map between the three extractions for the same ground: Portuguese/Dutch/English/French arrival → Lucent (Medieval file) + Parmar Part A; Carnatic Wars → Lucent (Medieval file) + Parmar Part A; Bengal/Mysore/Punjab → Parmar Part A + Lucent Part B.

**C2. Part A (Parmar) vs Part B (Lucent) — Bengal/Mysore/Punjab comparison**

- **Mir Jafar's restoration.** Parmar (p.156) lists "Mir Jafar was made the Nawab again" inside the 1765 Treaty-of-Allahabad terms. Lucent places Mir Jafar's _restoration_ right after Buxar (1764), lists his rule as 1764-65, and says that on his death his son **Nazmuddaula** signed the 20 Feb 1765 treaty, and that the Company acquired Diwani/Nizamat "from Nazmuddaula, the Nawab of Bengal" (Dual Government, p.69). ⚠ So the two books differ on _who_ was Nawab at the time of the Allahabad treaties (Parmar implies Mir Jafar; Lucent says Nazmuddaula). Left as printed in both; not reconciled.
- **Diwani grant vs Nizamat rights.** Parmar: Diwani of Bengal/Bihar/Orissa from Shah Alam II for Rs 26 lakh/year; Company to receive Rs 53 lakh "from the Emperor" for nizamat functions. Lucent: Company acquired both Diwani and Nizamat "from Nazmuddaula, the Nawab of Bengal" — no Rs figures, source of grant differs (Emperor vs Nawab). ⚠ Not reconciled.
- **Treaty of Allahabad terms.** Parmar gives details (Allahabad/Kara to Shah Alam II; Rs 50 lakh indemnity from Shuja-ud-Daula; Shah Alam under Company protection). Lucent gives only the fact and dates: two treaties, Shah Alam II (12 Aug 1765) and Shujauddaula (16 Aug 1765). No conflict; Parmar is the fuller source.
- **Battle of Buxar.** Both: 1764, Munro (Hector Munro in Parmar), Mir Qasim + Shuja-ud-daula + Shah Alam II. Consistent.
- **Mir Qasim.** Both: capital shifted Murshidabad → Munger; dastak dispute. Lucent adds cessions (Burdwan, Midnapore, Chittagong) and that he fled to Awadh; Parmar adds "ended Dastak issued by Farrukhsiyar" and "son-in-law of Mir Jafar" (Lucent also says son-in-law, in the Mir Jafar entry). Consistent.
- **Murshid Quli Khan's date.** Lucent text: appointed Subedar in **1717**, rule "1717-27"; Lucent's own annexation table: Nawab of Bengal founded **1713**. ⚠ Internal Lucent inconsistency (Parmar: 1717–1727). Left as printed.
- **Nawabs of Bengal chain.** Lucent gives the full chain (Murshid Quli Khan → Shujauddin → Sarfaraj Khan → Alivardi Khan → Sirajuddaula); Parmar's box omits Sarfaraj Khan. Not a conflict, but Lucent is the fuller source.
- **Golden Farman 1717.** Parmar only (Farrukhsiyar's farman, Rs 3,000/yr, dastaks). Lucent has no mention in Part B.
- **Black Hole Tragedy.** Parmar (p.156) only (1756). Lucent Part B does not use the term.
- **Plassey betrayal list.** Lucent only (Mir Jafar, Manikchand, Amichand, Jagat Seth, Khadim Khan). Parmar names only Mir Jafar. Plassey date: Lucent 23 June 1757 (Parmar gives year only).
- **First Anglo-Mysore War dates.** Parmar 1767-1769; Lucent **1766-69**. ⚠ One-year start-date difference; left as printed. Result: both say Mysore/Haidar Ali won; Treaty of Madras (1769).
- **Fourth Anglo-Mysore War dates.** Parmar 1798-1799; Lucent "1799". Minor.
- **Second Anglo-Mysore War cause.** Parmar: violation of the Madras Treaty by the British. Lucent: Warren Hastings attacked French port Mahe (in Haidar Ali's territory), Haidar led a joint front with Nizam and Marathas and captured Arcot. Different emphasis, not a contradiction.
- **Porto Novo 1781.** Both: Haidar Ali defeated by Eyre Coote. Consistent. (Lucent spells "Eyrecoot".)
- **Tipu Sultan facts.** Both: Treaty of Mangalore (1784) ended 2nd war; Treaty of Seringapatnam (1792) after 3rd war with Cornwallis; Tree of Liberty; Jacobin Club. Parmar adds Rs 3 crore indemnity, two sons as hostages, "Citizen Tipu", Napoleon contact; Lucent adds embassies to France/Turkey/Iran/Pegu and "only Indian ruler who understood importance of economic strength".
- **Punjab.** Parmar: Treaty of Amritsar (1809), Sutlej boundary, Duleep Singh, Treaties of Lahore & Bhairowal (1846), Treaty of Amritsar (1846: Kashmir → Gulab Singh for Rs 75 lakh), Aliwal (Sir Harry Smith), Mulraj/Multan, Kohinoor, Lord Hardinge/Dalhousie. Lucent: Ranjit Singh's career and conquests, successors, four battles of First Anglo-Sikh War (Mudki, Ferozshah, Aliwal, Sobraon), Treaty of Lahore 1846, Henry Lawrence as first resident, Dalhousie annexation (1849 per table), John Lawrence first chief commissioner. **Ranjit Singh's misl:** Parmar says "Sukerchakia Misl"; Lucent silent. **Treaty of Lahore:** both 1846.
- **Punjab annexation year.** Lucent table: 1849 (Dalhousie); Lucent text "Second Anglo-Sikh war (1848-49)". Parmar: 2nd Anglo-Sikh War (1848), does not give the annexation year. Consistent.
- **Subsidiary Alliance.** Lucent's table cites Subsidiary Alliance annexation years (Hyderabad 1798, Mysore 1799, Maratha/Carnatic 1801). Parmar p.157 gives Wellesley 1798 with adopters: Hyderabad 1798, Mysore 1799, Tanjore 1799, Awadh 1801, Peshwa 1802. ⚠ **Discrepancy:** Lucent's table has "Maratha-confederacy 1801 (Subsidiary Alliance)" and "Nawab of Carnatic/Arcot 1801"; Parmar has Peshwa **1802**, and does not list Carnatic. Left as printed. (Checked against Lucent's closing "Governor-General and Viceroys" section — governors_general_and_viceroys_raw_extraction.md Part B: it gives only "Nizam Ali (Nizam of Hyderabad) was the first Indian native ruler to accept the system of Subsidiary Alliance (1798)" under Wellesley; no further adopter list or years.)
- **Afghans / Burma / Nepal / Saragarhi.** Parmar Part A only in this chapter pair; Lucent's ch.16 has no Afghan/Burma/Nepal coverage; Lucent's closing "Governor-General and Viceroys" section (governors_general_and_viceroys_raw_extraction.md Part B) does carry short entries: 1st Anglo-Afghan War "(1836-42)" under Auckland (Parmar: 1838-1842), 2nd Afghan War 1878-80 and Gandamak 1879 under Lytton, Anglo-Nepalese War "(1813-23)" under Hastings (Parmar: Anglo-Nepal War 1814-16, Treaty of Sagauli), 1st Burmese War 1824-26 (Amherst), 2nd Burmese War 1852 (Dalhousie), 3rd Burmese War 1885 (Dufferin). ⚠ Dates for the Afghan and Nepal wars differ between the two books as noted.

**Governors-General and Viceroys — Raw Extraction**

Batch: Modern History raw-extraction (no test cross-referencing yet — Modern History's 14 Basic tests are not attempted). Format follows the Ancient History precedent (Sources → Part A/B content mirroring each book's own structure → cross-book notes), except that no "Test cross-reference index" section is included yet.

**Sources**

- **Parmar's SSC Fatman (2nd Ed., 2026)**, Modern History, ch.9 "Governor-General and Viceroy": book pp.185–186 (PDF 201–202; offset +16 in this stretch), chapter end confirmed by a red "■■■" marker at the bottom of the left column of p.186. Matches Parmar's printed Index (Modern History #9, "185-186").
- **Lucent's GK Book (2026)**, Indian History, closing section "Governor-General and Viceroys": book p.90 (lower half) – p.92 (PDF 100–102; offset +10 in this stretch), chapter end confirmed by a red "★★★" marker in the right column of p.92 (World History chapter begins on p.93 / PDF 103).

**Structure note**

Parmar gives a compact bullet list per ruler; Lucent gives a fuller narrative run with extra rulers and a "Governor Generals of Free India" section. Read fresh page by page; transcribed as printed, doubts flagged with ⚠ rather than corrected.

**PART A — Parmar's SSC Fatman (2nd Ed., 2026): "GOVERNOR-GENERAL AND VICEROY" (book pp.185–186, PDF 201–202)**

Running page header on both pages: "GOVERNOR-GENERAL AND VICEROY". Chapter closes with a red "■■■" end marker at the bottom of the left column of book p.186 (confirmed by direct read; the right column of p.186 is blank apart from faint show-through of the reverse side). Parmar's Index lists this chapter as "Governor-General and Viceroy 185–186"; Polity begins at p.187. Parmar's chapter map (PDF 170) lists its sub-headings as: Governor-Generals of India · Viceroys. Note that on p.185 the left-column banner "GOVERNOR-GENERALS OF INDIA" covers Warren Hastings → Dalhousie, and the right column continues Dalhousie's bullets and then has the banner "VICEROYS" (Canning → Curzon); p.186 continues Minto II → Mountbatten.

**\[book p.185 / PDF 201\] — left column**

\[Banner\] **GOVERNOR-GENERALS OF INDIA**

**Lord Warren Hastings (1773-1785)**

- Regulating Act (1773) → 1st Governor General of Bengal.
- Pitt's India Act (1784).
- 1st Anglo-Maratha War (1775-1782) → Treaty of Salbai signed (1782)
- 2nd Anglo-Mysore War (1780-1784).
- Asiatic Society of Bengal (1784) formed.

**Lord Cornwallis (1786-1793)**

- 3rd Anglo-Mysore War (1790-1792) → Treaty of Seringpatnam.
- Permanent Settlement (1793)
- Death: India (tomb in Ghazipur, Uttar Pradesh).
- He is known as Father of Indian Civil Services.

**Lord Wellesley (1798-1805)**

- 2nd Anglo-Maratha War (1803-1805).
- 4th Anglo-Mysore War (1799).
- Treaty of Bassein (1802).

**Lord Minto (1807-1813)**

- Treaty of Amritsar (1809) signed between Maharaja Ranjit Singh and Britishers.

**Lord Hastings (1813-1823)**

- 3rd Anglo-Maratha War (1817-1819).
- Anglo-Nepal War (1814-1816) → Treaty of Sagauli signed.
- Ryotwari System introduced by Munro and Reed.

**Lord William Bentinck (1828-1835)**

- First Governor-General of India.
- Abolition of Sati (1829).
- Suppression of Thugs.
- He abolished Circuit Courts.
- He is known as Father of Modern Education in India → Macaulay's Minute (1835) during his tenure.

**Lord Metcalfe (1835-1836)**

- He is known as the Liberator of Indian Press.

**Lord Auckland (1836-1842)**

- 1st Anglo-Afghan War (1838-1842).

**Lord Hardinge (1844-1848)**

- 1st Anglo-Sikh War → Treaty of Lahore signed.

**Lord Dalhousie (1848-1856)**

- Doctrine of Lapse (1848).
- 1st railway line laid down (1853) → from Bombay to Thane (34 km).
- Widow Remarriage Act (1856).

**\[book p.185 / PDF 201\] — right column**

- Postal Act Telegraph lines spread across.
- Wood's Dispatch (1854) → Magna Carta of Indian Education.
- He founded Public Works Department (PWD).
- 2nd Anglo-Sikh War (1848-1849).

\[Banner\] **VICEROYS**

**Lord Canning (1856-1862)**

- He became the 1st Viceroy of India.
- 1857 Revolt.
- Government of India Act (1858) was passed.

**Lord Mayo (1869-1872)**

- 1st Unsynchronised (not complete) Census was held during his tenure (1872).
- He established Statistical Survey of India.
- Sher Ali Afridi killed him in Andaman Jail.

**Lord Lytton (1876-1880)**

- Arms Act (1878).
- He brought Vernacular Press Act (1878) to ban local newspapers.
- First Delhi Durbar (1877) to proclaim Queen Victoria as Empress of India.
- 2nd Afghan War (1878-1880).
- He lowered the maximum age of eligibility for Civil Services (reduced the age from 21 to 19 years).
- \[Box\] _"Bengal Gazette" by James Augustus Hickey was the 1st Newspaper in India (1780)._

**Lord Ripon (1880-1884)**

- Ilbert bill was introduced in 1883.
- He repealed Vernacular Press Act.
- 1st Synchronised (complete) Census held during his tenure (1881).
- He is known as Father of Local Self-Government.
- Hunter Commission (1882) was setup which was related to Education.
- 1st Factory Act (1881).

**Lord Dufferin (1884-1888)**

- Formation of INC (1885).

**Lord Curzon (1899-1905)**

- Partition of Bengal (1905).
- Indian Universities Act.
- Calcutta Corporation Act.
- Curzon-Kitchner controversy.
- Younghusband Mission (to Tibet).

**\[book p.186 / PDF 202\] — left column**

**Lord Minto II (1905-1910)**

- Muslim League formation (1906) by Aga Khan.
- Surat Split.

**Lord Hardinge II (1910-1916)**

- 3rd Delhi Durbar for King George (1911).
- During his tenure, capital shifted from Calcutta to Delhi.
- Royal Commission on Civil Services was appointed in 1912.

**Lord Chelmsford (1916-1921)**

- Government of India Act, also known as Montagu-Chelmsford Reforms (1919).
- Rowlatt Act (1919).
- Jallianwala Bagh Massacre (1919).

**Lord Reading (1921-1926)**

- Chauri Chaura Incident (1922).
- Kakori Train Incident (1925).
- Establishment of Swaraj Party (1923).
- Repeal of Press Act (1910) and Rowlatt Act (1919).

**Lord Irwin (1926-1931)**

- Visit of Simon Commission (1928).
- Lahore Session of Congress (1929).
- Dandi March (1930).
- 1st Round Table Conference.

**Lord Willingdon (1931-1936)**

- 2nd Round Table Conference.
- 3rd Round Table Conference.
- Government of India Act (1935).

**Lord Linlithgow (1936-1944)**

- World War II.
- August Offer (1940).
- Individual Satyagrah (1940).
- Quit India Movement (1942).
- Cripps Mission (1942).

**Lord Wavell (1944-1947)**

- CR Formula (1944).
- Wavell Plan (1945).
- Cabinet Mission Proposals (1946).
- Observance of Direct Action Day (16 August 1946).

**Lord Mountbatten (1947-48)**

- → Last British Governor General of India.
- _Only Indian Governor General of India: C. Rajagopalachari._

■■■ \[chapter end marker, red\]

**PART B — Lucent's GK Book (2026): "Governor-General and Viceroys" (book p.90 lower half – p.92, PDF 100–102)**

This is the closing section of Lucent's "Indian History" chapter (the chapter ends with a "★★★" marker in the middle of the right column of book p.92, followed by the start of the "World History" chapter on p.93). It comes after the reference tables (Important Places, Travellers, Names, Sayings, Battles, Reforms/Acts, Committees/Commissions, Congress Sessions) — those tables are in lucent_indian_history_reference_tables_raw_extraction.md. Sub-headings as printed: "Governor-General and Viceroys" → "Governors of Bengal (1757-74)" → "Governor-Generals of Bengal (1774-1833)" → "Governor Generals of India (1833-58)" → "Governor Generals and Viceroys (1858-1947)" → "Governor Generals of Free India (1947-50)". Each ruler's name is printed in a red colour and entries are running prose; "Wars :" / "Revenue Reforms :" / "Judicial Reforms :" etc. are printed as run-in labels.

**\[book p.90 / PDF 100\] — left column, lower half**

**Governor-General and Viceroys**

**Governors of Bengal (1757-74)**

_Robert Clive :_ Governor of Bengal during 1757-60 and again during 1765-67, Established Dual Government in Bengal from 1765-72.

_Vanisttart (1760-65) :_ The Battle of Buxar (1764).

_Cartier (1769-72) :_ Bengal Famine (1770).

_Warren Hastings (1772-74) :_ Abolished Dual Government (1772).

**Governor-Generals of Bengal (1774-1833)**

_Warren Hastings (1774-85) :_ Became Governor-General in 1774 through the Regulating Act, 1773; Wrote introduction to the first English translation of the 'Gita' by Charles Wilkins; Founded the Asiatic Society of Bengal with William Jones in 1784.

_Revenue Reforms :_ Auctioned the right to collect land revenue to the highest bidder; Divided Bengal into districts and appointed collectors and other revenue officials.

_Judicial Reforms :_ Started Diwani and Faujdari adalats at the district level and Sadar diwani and Nizamat adalats _(appellate courts)_ at Calcutta; Redefined Hindu and Muslim laws; A translation of the code in Sanskrit appeared in 1776 under the title of "Code of Gentoo laws".

_Wars :_ Rohilla War (1774); 1st Anglo-Maratha War (1776-82); 2nd Anglo-Mysore War (1780-84).

**\[book p.90 / PDF 100\] — right column**

_Lord Cornwallis (1786-93) :_ First person to codify laws in 1793. The code separated the revenue administration from the administration of justice; Created the post of district judge; Introduced Permanent Settlement in Bengal (1793); Cornwallis is called 'the Father of Civil Service in India'.

_Wars :_ 3rd Anglo-Mysore War _(defeat of Tipu and the Treaty of Seringapatanam, 1792)_

_Sir John Shore (1793-98) :_ Introduced the 1st Charter Act (1793).

_Wars :_ Battle of Kharda between Nizam and the Marathas (1795)

_Lord Wellesley (1798-1805) :_ Started Subsidiary Alliance system to achieve British paramountcy in India, Nizam Ali _(Nizam of Hyderabad)_ was the first Indian native ruler to accept the system of Subsidiary Alliance (1798); Madras Presidency was formed during his tenure.

_Wars :_ 4th Anglo-Mysore War (1799)—defeat and the death of Tipu Sultan; 2nd Anglo -Maratha War (1803-05)-defeat of the Scindia, the Bhonsle and the Holkar; Treaty of Bassein (1802).

_George Barlow (1805-1807) :_ Vellore Mutiny (1806)

_Lord Minto I (1807-1813) :_ Concluded Treaty of Amritsar with Ranjit Singh (1809); Charter Act of 1813 was passed.

_Lord Hastings (1813-1823) :_ Adopted the policy of intervention and war.

_Wars :_ Anglo-Nepalese War (1813-23); 3rd Anglo-Maratha War (1817-18). Hastings forced humiliating treaties on Peshwa and the Scindia; Introduced the Ryotwari settlement in Madras by Thomas Munro, the Governor.

_Lord Amherst (1823-28) : Wars :_ 1st Burmese War (1824-26); Acquisition of territories in Malay Peninsula; Capture of Bharatpur (1826).

_Lord W. Bentick (1828-33) :_ Most liberal and enlightened Governor-General of India; Regarded as 'the Father of Modern Western Education in India'; Abolished Sati and other cruel rites (1829); Annexation of Mysore (1831). Concluded a treaty of perpetual friendship with Ranjit Singh (1831); Passed the Charter Act of 1833, which provided that no Indian subject of Company was to be debarred from holding an office on account of his religion, place of birth, descent and colour.

**Governor Generals of India (1833-58)**

_Lord W. Bentick (1833-35) :_ Macaulay's minutes on education were accepted declaring that English should be the official language of India, Macaulay's Minute of 1835 introduced English education in India with the declared object of producing 'a class of persons, Indian in blood and colour, but English in tastes'; Abolished provincial courts of appeal and circuit set up by Cornwallis, Appointment of commissioners of revenue and circuit.

_Wars :_ Annexed Coorg (1834), Central Cachar (1834) on the plea of misgovernance.

_Sir Charles Metcalfe (1835-1836) :_ Passed the famous Press Law, which liberated the press in India.

_Lord Auckland (1836-42) : 1st Anglo-Afghan War (1836-42)—great blow to the prestige of the British in India._ \[right edge of column slightly cut off in the scan: "Wa…", "Ind…" — read as above from context; ⚠ a few word-ends unreadable\]

**\[book p.91 / PDF 101\] — left column**

_Lord Ellenborough (1842-44) :_ Brought an end to the Afghan War; Annexation of Sindh (1843); War with Gwalior (1843); Abolished slavery (1843).

_Lord Hardinge I (1844-48) :_ 1st Anglo-Sikh war (1845-46) and the Treaty of Lahore, 1846 (marked the end of Sikh sovereignty in India); Gave preference to English educated in employment.

_Lord Dalhousie (1848-56) :_ Original Name–James Andrew Broun Ramsay, Abolished titles and pensions, Introduction of Widow Remarriage Bill (Nov. 17,1855).

_Wars :_ Introduced Doctrine of Lapse (Captured Satara (1848), Jaitpur and Sambhalpur (1849), Baghat (1850), Udaipur (1852), Jhansi (1853) and Nagpur (1854); Fought 2nd Anglo-Sikh War (1848-49) and annexed the whole of the Punjab; 2nd Anglo-Burmese War (1852) and annexation of Lower Burma or Pegu; Annexation of Berar in 1853; Annexation of Avadh in 1856 on charges of mal-administration.

_Administrative Reforms :_ Introduced the system of centralized control in the newly acquired territories known as Non-Regulation System; Raised Gurkha regiments.

_Educational Reforms :_ Recommended the Thomsonian System of Vernacular education for the whole of the Northwestern Provinces (1853); Wood's Educational Despatch of 1854 and opening of Anglo-Vernacular Schools and Government Colleges; An Engineering College was established at Roorkee.

_Public Works :_ Started the first railway line in 1853 _(connecting Bombay with Thana);_ Started electric telegraph service. Laid the basis of the modern postal system (1854); A separate public works department was set up for the first time; Started work on the Grand Trunk Road and developed the harbours of Karachi, Bombay and Calcutta.

_Lord Canning (1856-58) :_ The last Governor General of India; Passed Widow Remarriage Act (July 25, 1856); Revolt of 1857; Passed the Act of 1858, which ended the rule of the East India Company. Withdrew Doctrine of Lapse.

**Governor Generals and Viceroys (1858-1947)**

_Lord Canning (1858-62) :_ The Indian Councils Act of 1861 was passed, which proved to be a landmark in the constitutional history of India; The Indian Penal Code of Criminal Procedure (1859) was passed; The Indian High Court Act (1861) was enacted; Income Tax was introduced for the first time in 1858; The universities of Calcutta, Bombay and Madras founded in 1857; The Indigo riots in Bengal (1860).

_Lord Elgin I (1862-63) :_ Wahabi Movement _(Pan-Islamic Movement)_

_Sir John Lawrence (1864-69) :_ Telegraphic communication was opened with Europe; High Courts were established at Calcutta, Bombay and Madras in 1865; Expanded canal works and railways; Bhutan War (1865); Advocated State-managed railways; Created the Indian Forests Department and reorganized the native judicial service.

_Lord Mayo (1869-72) :_ Introduced financial decentralization in India, Established Mayo College at Ajmer for the princes; Organised the Statistical Survey of India,

**\[book p.91 / PDF 101\] — right column**

Established the Department of Agriculture and Commerce, Introduction of State Railways. He was the only Viceroy to be murdered in office by a convict in Andamans in 1872.

_Lord Northbrook (1872-76) :_ Kuka Movement of Punjab took rebellious turn during his period.

_Lord Lytton (1876-80) :_ Most infamous Governor-General, Pursued free trade and abolished duties on 29 British manufactured goods which accelerated the drain of wealth of the India, Arranged the Grand Durbar in Delhi (in 1877) when the country was suffering from a severe famine; Passed the Royal Title Act (1876) and Queen Victoria was declared as the Kaisar-i-Hind i.e. Empress of India; Arms Act (1878) made mandatory for Indians to acquire license for arms; Passed the infamous Vernacular Press Act (1878); Proposed the plan of Statutory Civil Service in 1878-79 and lowered the maximum age limit from 21 to 19 years, the 2nd Afghan war (1878-80) proved a Sucess for British and the treaty of Gandmak (1879) converted Afghanistan into an almost protected state of India.

_Lord Ripon (1880-84) :_ Repeal of the Vernacular Press Act, 1882; The First Factory Act, 1881 to improve labour condition, Resolution of Local Self Government in 1882, Resolution on Land Revenue Policy; Appointed Hunter Commission _(for education reforms)_ in 1882; The Ilbert Bill controversy erupted during his time (1883).

_Lord Dufferin (1884-88) :_ 3rd Burmese War (Annexation of Upper and Lower Burma in 1885, Establishment of Indian National Congress in 1885).

_Lord Lansdowne (1888-94) :_ The Factory Act of 1891; Categorization of Civil Services into imperial, provincial and subordinate; Indian Council Act of 1892 _(introduced elections which was indirect);_ Appointment of the Durand Commission to define the line between British India and Afghanistan (1893).

_Lord Elgin II (1894-99) :_ The Munda Uprising (Birsa Munda) of 1899, Convention delimiting the frontier between China and India was ratified, Great famine of 1896-97, Lyall Commission appointed after famine (1897), Assassination of two British officials–Rand and Amherst–by Chapekar Brothers in 1897.

_Lord Curzon (1899-1905) :_ Appointed a Police Commission in 1902 under Andrew Frazer; Set up the Universities Commission and accordingly the Indian Universities Act of 1904 was passed; Set up the Department of Commerce and Industry; Calcutta Corporation Act (1899); Passed the Indian Coinage and Paper Currency Act _(in 1899)_ and put India on a gold standard; Partition of Bengal took place in 1905 _(It was cardinal blunder of Curzon);_ The idea to build Victoria Memorial _(Calcutta)_ was conceived by Lord Curzon. The foundation stone of the memorial was laid in 1906 and it was opened for public in 1921.

_Lord Minto II (1905-10) :_ Swadeshi Movement (1905-08); Foundation of the Muslim League, 1906; Surat session and split in the Congress (1907), Newspapers Act, 1908; Morley-Minto Reforms, 1909.

_Lord Hardinge (1910-16) :_ Annulment of the Partition of Bengal (1911), Transfer of capital from Calcutta to Delhi (1911-announcement, 1912-Came into effect); Delhi Darbar and Coronation of King George V and Queen Mary (1911);

**\[book p.92 / PDF 102\] — left column**

Establishment of Hindu Mahasabha by Madan Mohan Malviya (1915).

_Lord Chelmsford (1916-21) :_ Home Rule Movement launched by Tilak and Annie Besant (1916); Lucknow Pact between Congress and Muslim League (1916); Arrival of Gandhi in India (1915); Champaran Satyagraha (1917); Montague's August Declaration (1917); Kheda Satyagraha and Satyagraha at Ahmedabad (1918); Government of India Act (1919), Repressive Rowlatt Act (1919); Jalianwala Bagh Massacre (April 13, 1919), Appointment of Hunter Commission to probe Jalianwala Bagh Massacre (Oct. 19, 1919), Khilafat Movement (1920-22); Non-Cooperation Movement (1920-22).

_Lord Reading (1921-26) :_ Criminal Law Amendment Act and abolition of cotton excise; Repeal of Press Act of 1910 and Rowlatt Act of 1919; Violent Moplah rebellion in Kerala (1921); Foundation of CPI (1921); Chauri Chaura incident (1922); Foundation of Swaraj Party (1923); Kakori Train Dacoity (1925); Foundation of RSS (1925); Murder of Swami Shradhanand (1926).

_Lord Irwin (1926-31) :_ Simon Commission announced in 1927; Butler Commission (1927); Nehru Report (1928); 14 points of Jinnah (1929); Lahore session of Congress and 'Poorna Swaraj' declaration (1929); Civil Disobedience Movement (1930); Dandi March (1930); Ist Round Table Conference (1930); Gandhi-Irwin Pact (1931).

_Lord Willingdon (1931-36) :_ IInd Round Table Conference (1931); Civil Disobedience Movement (1932); Announcement of MacDonald's Communal Award (1932); IIIrd Round Table Conference; Foundation of Congress Socialist Party — CSP (1934); Government of India Act (1935); Burma separated from India (1935); All India Kisan Sabha (1936).

**\[book p.92 / PDF 102\] — right column (upper part)**

_Lord Linlithgow (1936-43) :_ General Election (1936-37); Congress ministries formed in 1937 and Resignation of Congress ministries in 1939; 'Deliverance Day' celebrated by Muslim League in 1939; Foundation of forward Block by S.C. Bose (1939); Lahore Resolution (1940); August Offer (1940); Cripps Mission (1942); Quit India Movement (1942).

_Lord Wavell (1943-1947) :_ C. R. Formula 1944; Wavell Plan and Simla Conference in 1945; End of IInd World War in 1945; INA Trials in 1945; Naval mutiny in 1946; Cabinet Mission, 1946 and acceptance of its proposals by Congress; Direct Action Day by the Muslim League on 16th August, 1946.

_Lord Mountbatten (Mar-Aug 1947) :_ Announced the 3 June, 1947 Plan; Introduction of Indian Independence Bill in the House of Commons; Appointment of 2 boundary commissions under Sir Cyril Radcliffe.

**Governor Generals of Free India (1947-50)**

_Lord Mountbatten (1947-48) :_ The first Governor General of free India; Kashmir acceded to India (Oct., 1947); Murder of Gandhi (Jan. 30, 1948).

_C. Rajagopalachari (June 1948 – Jan. 25, 1950) :_ The last Governor General of free India; The only Indian Governor-General.

★★★ \[end of chapter marker, red\]

- **Lucent** (Part B, book pp.90–92): a longer, prose-style run with six headings — Governors of Bengal (1757-74), Governor-Generals of Bengal (1774-1833), Governor Generals of India (1833-58), Governor Generals and Viceroys (1858-1947), Governor Generals of Free India (1947-50). It contains rulers Parmar omits (see C3). Lucent's reference tables that pair with this section (Reforms/Acts with viceroy names; Committees/Commissions by viceroy) are in lucent_indian_history_reference_tables_raw_extraction.md.

**Quit India Movement & the Road to Independence (Communal Award → Partition; Parmar ch.8) — Raw Extraction**

Batch: Modern History raw-extraction (no test cross-referencing yet — Modern History's 14 Basic tests are not attempted). Format follows the Ancient History precedent (Sources → Part A/B content mirroring each book's own structure → cross-book notes), except that no "Test cross-reference index" section is included yet.

**Sources**

- **Parmar's SSC Fatman (2nd Ed., 2026)**, Modern History, ch.8 "Quit India Movement": book pp.180–184 (PDF 196–200; offset +16 in this stretch), chapter end confirmed by a red "■■■" marker in the left column of p.184. Matches Parmar's printed Index (Modern History #8, "180-184").
- **Lucent's GK Book (2026)**: no separate section — counterpart passages are inside "19. IV. The Gandhian Era (1917-47)" (book pp.79–82) and are transcribed in gandhian_era_raw_extraction.md (Part B) — cross-referenced, not re-transcribed (Part B below lists exactly where).

**Structure note**

Parmar's ch.8 is titled "Quit India Movement" but actually covers the whole run from the Communal Award (1932) through Independence, Gandhi's assassination and a few post-Independence items (Polo, Kashmir, 1971/1972 boxes). Lucent's counterpart is the tail of its continuous Gandhian-era section. Read fresh page by page; transcribed as printed, doubts flagged with ⚠ rather than corrected.

**PART A — Parmar's SSC Fatman (2nd Ed., 2026): "QUIT INDIA MOVEMENT" (book pp.180–184, PDF 196–200)**

Running page header on all five pages: "QUIT INDIA MOVEMENT". Chapter closes with a red "■■■" end marker in the left column of book p.184 after the Noakhali/15 August 1947 bullet (confirmed by direct read; the right column of p.184 is blank). Parmar's Index lists this chapter as "Quit India Movement 180–184". Parmar's chapter map (PDF 170) lists this chapter's sub-headings as: Communal Award · Congress Sessions · Cripps Mission 1942 · Indian National Army (INA) · Mountbatten Plan · Government of India Act 1935 · August Offer 1940 · Quit India Movement 1942 · Cabinet Mission Plan. The chapter, as printed, is much wider than its title (Communal Award through Independence, Gandhi's assassination and post-Independence items). Page 184 has photographs and meme-style captions, marked as such below.

**\[book p.180 / PDF 196\] — left column**

\[Banner\] **COMMUNAL AWARDS**

- The Communal Award was announced by _British Prime Minister Ramsay MacDonald on 16th August 1932._ It is also known as _MacDonald Award._
- It recognised the _"Depressed Classes"_ as minorities and _provided them a separate electorate_ along with some other communities like Muslims, Sikhs, Christians, Europeans, and Marathas as well.
- _Mahatma Gandhi strongly opposed_ the separate electorate for Depressed classes, viewing it as a threat to Hindu unity however _B. R. Ambedkar supported_ the Award, as it ensured political representation and safeguards the rights of the Depressed classes.
- \[Box\] _Congress neither accepted nor rejected the Communal Award._

**Poona Pact 1932**

- The Poona Pact was an agreement between _Mahatma Gandhi and B. R. Ambedkar,_ signed on _24th September 1932,_ at _Yerwada Central Jail_ in _Pune._
- While in prison, Gandhi announced a _fast unto death_ till the time separate electorates were removed from the Award, which led to an agreement between Gandhi and Ambedkar that discarded the separate electorate.

**Key Provisions of Poona Pact**

- \[Box\] **Note:** _Madan Mohan Malaviya signed Poona Pact on behalf of Gandhi._
- No separate electorates for Depressed Classes.
- Number of seats reserved for Depressed Classes would be increased.

**Gandhi's Efforts against Untouchability**

- _All India Anti-Untouchability League (1932):_ This League, founded by Mahatma Gandhi was later re-named as the _Harijan Sevak Sangh._
- Harijan (Weekly newspaper).

\[Banner\] **GOVERNMENT OF INDIA ACT 1935**

**1937 Elections**

- Congress formed ministries in all _7 out of 11 provinces_ (later 8) except in:
  - _Punjab:_ Unionist Party.
  - _Bengal:_ Krishak Praja Party + Muslim League coalition.
  - _Sindh:_ United Party (Coalition).
- \*For provisions of GOI Act 1935 refer to chapter: "Important Acts" in Polity section.

\[Banner\] **CONGRESS SESSIONS**

- _1934:_ Congress Socialist Party was formed by J PNarayan, Ram Manohar Lohia, A N Dev, Minoo Masani.
- _1936:_ Held in Lucknow; All India Kisan Sabha (AIKS) was formed by Swami Sahajanand Saraswati.

**\[book p.180 / PDF 196\] — right column**

- _1937:_ Faizpur → 1st session to be held in a village (Jalgaon, Maharashtra). This session was presided over by J L Nehru.
- _1938:_ Haripura, Gujarat led by _S C Bose (elected as President of INC)_ marked the beginning of growing ideological differences between Bose and Mahatma Gandhi, though the full rift developed in 1939 during the Tripuri Session.
- _National Planning Committee (NPC)_ was established in _1938_ by _S C Bose,_ during the _Haripura Session._ The _first chairman_ of this Committee was _J L Nehru._
- _1939:_ Tripuri Session in Jabalpur (Madhya Pradesh) → _S C Bose was re-elected as President_ of INC by defeating Gandhi's candidate _Pattabhi Sitaramayya,_ but later _S C Bose resigned_ from Congress and he formed _All India Forward Bloc, 1939 in Unnao, Uttar Pradesh_ (as a faction within the Congress).

**World War II**

- In _1939,_ World War II began → _It was fought between the Allied Powers_ (Britain, France, USA, USSR) and the _Axis Powers_ (German, Italy, Japan).
- In _October 1939,_ the INC resigned from all provincial ministries to show protest against Britain's decision to _involve India in World War II without consulting Indian leaders._
- After resigning, the INC made offers to _Viceroy Lord Linlithgow_ regarding India's involvement in World War II.

_The demands were:_

1. Demand for establishment of Constituent Assembly to draft a Constitution for the Indian people.
2. Demand for establishment of fully responsible Government at both Provincial and Central level.

- \[Box\] _Autobiography of Hitler: Mein Kampf_
- \[Box\] _The Muslim League observed 22 December 1939 as the "Day of Deliverance" (Youm-e-Najat) to celebrate the resignation of the Congress governments._

\[Banner\] **AUGUST OFFER 1940**

- In _1940, August Offer_ was sent by Lord Linlithgow under the direction of _British Prime Minister Winston Churchill_ to secure India's cooperation in World War II. The key Provisions were:
  1. The British Government promised to grant Dominion Status for India.
  2. Indians could create their own Constituent Assembly themselves with inclusion of more Indian representatives than whites.
  3. An advisory war council was to be established.
  4. Veto for Minorities: No future constitution would be adopted without the consent of the minorities.

**\[book p.181 / PDF 197\] — left column**

→ However these provisions were rejected by Congress as _"Purna Swaraj"_ as their goal. → Jawaharlal Nehru completely rejected the British August Offer of 1940, calling it _"dead as a doornail"._ → The Muslim League also rejected this proposal.

**Individual Satyagrahi 1940**

- The INC decided to launch the _"Individual Satyagraha"_ after the failure of August Offer under the leadership of _Mahatma Gandhi_ in _1940_ with _Vinoba Bhave_ nominated as _first Satyagrahi, J L Nehru as second_ and _Brahma Dutt as the third._
- _Objective:_ To protest against India's forced participation in World War II and to assert the right to freedom of speech, without causing mass disruption to British war efforts.
- \[Box\] _Revolutionary Socialist Party was established in 1940._

\[Banner\] **CRIPPS MISSION 1942**

_Background/Reasons (1941):_

- _Threat of Japan:_ Rapid Japanese expansion in Southeast Asia and advance up to Burma created fear of invasion of India.
- _International Pressure:_ After the Pearl Harbor attack, Franklin D. Roosevelt and Allied nations pressured Britain to grant political concessions to India.
- _Attack on Russia:_ Operation Barbarossa by Germany against the Soviet Union (1941) worsened Britain's war position, increasing its urgent need for Indian support (men and resources).
- In _1942, Stafford Cripps_ was sent to India with a constitutional reforms. The key proposals were:
  1. A Constituent Assembly would be formed to frame a new constitution for the country. This Assembly would have members elected by the provincial assemblies and also nominated by Princely States.
  2. India would be granted Dominion Status, allowing self-governance while remaining part of the British Commonwealth.
  3. Any province unwilling to join the Indian dominion could form a separate union and have a separate constitution.
  4. The transfer of power and the rights of minorities would be safeguarded.

The proposals were rejected by INC. Gandhi famously referred Cripps offer of Dominion Status as _"post-dated cheque drawn on a failing bank"._

\[Banner\] **QUIT INDIA MOVEMENT**

- _July 1942: The Congress Working Committee_ met at _Wardha, Maharashtra_ where _Quit India Resolution_ was adopted. It was ratified at _Gowalika Tank, Bombay._

**\[book p.181 / PDF 197\] — right column**

**Quit India Movement**

- On _8th August 1942, Mahatma Gandhi launched Quit India Movement_ (also known as _August Kranti_) in response to the failure of Cripps Mission. Which was initially not supported by J L Nehru and Hindu Mahasabha.
- The _"Do or Die"_ slogan was given _during a speech by Mahatma Gandhi_ at the _Gowalia Tank Maidan_ or _August Kranti Maidan_ in Mumbai. The slogan was a call to action for Indians to fight for independence from British rule.
- The very first day all leaders got arrested and that is why this movement was a _leaderless movement._
- _Aruna Asaf Ali_ presided over Congress Working Committee, she also hoisted the Indian Flag at the Gowalika Tank in Mumbai.

**Specific Appeals made by Gandhi (during the Quit India Movement):**

- To _government servants_ Gandhi advised not to resign from their posts but instead urged them to remain loyal to the cause of the INC and work against British policies subtly.
- _Soldiers_ were instructed not to resign from their duties but were urged to refuse orders to fire on fellow compatriots.
- Gandhi appealed to _peasants_ to withhold rent payments if the landlords or Zamindars were loyal to the British government.
- He encouraged the _people living in princely states_ to support their ruler if the ruler was anti-British. If the ruler was pro-British, Gandhi advised them to peacefully oppose the government.

**Martyrs**

- _Kushal Konwar_ was the only martyr of the Quit India phase of the freedom movement who was hanged.
- _Kanaklata Barua_ was shot dead by the British forces while leading a procession during QIM.

**Underground Activities Carried Out:**

- _Usha Mehta:_ Launched _underground radio in Bombay_ and appealed Indians to join Quit India Movement
- _Parallel government_ were setup:
  - _In Ballia by: Chittu Pandey_
  - _In Tamluk by: Jatiya Sarkar_
  - _In Satara by: Prati Sarkar (Y B Chauhan, Nana Patil)_
- _23 March 1943: Pakistan Day was observed by Muslim League._

_Jinnah's Reaction:_

- _Sarojini Naidu_ called Jinnah the _Father of Hindu-Muslim unity_ after the Lucknow Pact (1916).
- Due to disagreement (especially with Gandhiji's Non-Cooperation Movement), Jinnah left the Congress for some time and went to London.

**\[book p.182 / PDF 198\] — left column**

- Upon returning to India, he reorganized the Muslim League and appealed for a separate nation as Pakistan through the Lahore Resolution of 1940.

**Pakistan Movement**

- The word "Pakistan" was coined by _Rahmat Ali_ in 1933.

**Lahore Resolution (1940):**

- It is also called the Pakistan Resolution.
- It was a formal political statement adopted by the All India Muslim League on the occasion of its three-day general session in Lahore, Punjab, from 22 to 24 March 1940.
- A separate homeland for the Muslims of British India was demanded.
- On _23 March 1943 Pakistan Day_ was observed by the Muslim League.

**Mediation between Congress and Muslim League**

- _1944:_ C. Rajagopalachari gave _C R Formula_ (Rajagopalachari Formula) in response to the growing tensions between the INC and the Muslim League.
- _Desai Liaqat Pact 1945:_ It was an unsuccessful agreement between _Bhulabhai Desai_ (from Congress) and _Liaqat Ali Khan_ (from Muslim League).
- _1945: Wavell Plan_ was proposed by then _Viceroy Lord Archibald Wavell_ as an attempt to resolve the political deadlock between the _INC_ and the _Muslim League._ This plan was discussed in Shimla Conference; however it failed.

\[Box\] _In September 1945, World War II completely ended with the US bombings. In August 1945, US bombed in Hiroshima and Nagasaki (two cities of Japan) on 6 August and 9 August respectively. The bomb dropped on Hiroshima was known as "Little Boy". While the bomb dropped on Nagasaki was known as "Fat Man" which was more powerful than the "Little Boy" and that is from where we came up with the idea of naming this book and our GK MCQ Book (Little Boy)._

\[Banner\] **INDIAN NATIONAL ARMY (INA)**

- The _Indian National Army (INA)_ was founded during World War II to fight against British rule in India.
- It was initially formed in _1942_ by _Mohan Singh with Indian Prisoners of Wars in Singapore_ (it was later handed to _Rash Behari Bose_ who again transferred the leadership to _S C Bose_).
- \[Box\] _Birthday anniversary of S.C. Bose is celebrated every year as "Parakram Divas"._

**Subhash Chandra Bose**

- He was born in _Cuttack_ in _23rd Jan 1897._
- In _1942,_ he met _Hitler_ in Germany using a pseudo name _"Orlando Mazzotta"._
- The Indian National Army (Azad Hind Fauj) was reorganised by Bose in _Singapore_ in _1943._
- \[Image: portrait of S.C. Bose in cap\]

**\[book p.182 / PDF 198\] — right column**

- He founded _"Free India Legion/Tiger Legion"_ in 1941 in _Berlin, Germany._
- He formed a women regiment named after _Rani Lakshmibai_ led by Captain Lakshmi Swaminathan.
- _14 April 1944,_ Colonel _Shaukat Ali Malik_ of the Azad Hind Fauj (INA) hoisted India's tricolour flag in _Moirang, Manipur,_ with the help of Japan. (This was the first time the tricolour was hoisted on Indian soil by the Azad Hind Fauj.)
- On _15th August,_ Japan (Emperor Hirohito) surrendered to Allied powers, marking the end of World War II.
- _He died on: 18th August_ in a plane crash.
- S C Bose was called as _"Patriot of Patriots"_ by Gandhi.
- In _1944,_ he was the person to give _"Father of Nation"_ title to Gandhi through Singapore Radio.

**Famous Slogans by S C Bose**

- _"Delhi Chalo"_
- _"Give me blood, and I shall give you freedom"_
- _"Jai Hind"_
- \[Box\] _The Indian struggle 1920-1942 was authorised by S C Bose._

**INA trials**

- The Indian National Army trials (also known as the INA trials and the Red Fort trials) was the British Indian trial by court martial of a number of officers of the Indian National Army (INA) between _November 1945_ and _May 1946._
- _First INA trial was held against: Prem Kumar Sehgal, Shah Nawaz Khan_ and _Gurbaksh Singh Dhillon_ in _Red Fort_ in _1945._
- Congress came in defence of these INA soldiers and formed a committee which included Jawaharlal Nehru, Bhulabhai Desai, Asaf Ali, Tej Bahadur Sapru, Kailash Nath Katju, and others.
- _18th February 1946:_ The _ratings_ (sailors in lowest rank) of _Royal Indian Navy (RIN)_ revolted against British due to the discrimination and poor conditions and this led to a historic _Royal Indian Navy Mutiny._ This event began at the _HMS Talwar_ (a shore establishment in Bombay) and spread to Calcutta, Karachi and Bombay, against the _poor working conditions_ (low pay, poor food quality, etc.).
  - → The mutiny was called off at the behest of the Congress and the Muslim League.

\[Banner\] **CABINET MISSION PLAN**

- In _1945,_ elections were held in Britain where _Labour Party_ under the leadership of _Clement Atlee_ formed the government.
- In _March 1946, Clement Attlee (then PM of Britain)_ sent a delegation called the Cabinet Mission consisting of:
- _Lord Pethick-Lawrence (Chairman), Sir Stafford Cripps_ and _A. V. Alexander._

**\[book p.183 / PDF 199\] — left column**

- _Objective:-_ Transfer of power and constitution-making.

**Provisions in Cabinet Mission Plan**

- The plan proposed a united India, rejecting the demand for a separate Pakistan.
- The Cabinet Mission Plan proposed a three-tier administrative structure for British India:
  - _Federal Union_ at the top level,
  - _Groups of provinces_ at the middle level,
  - _Individual provinces_ at the bottom level.
- _Provincial were divided into three groups:_
  - _Group A:_ Hindu-majority provinces (Madras, Bombay, UP, Bihar, Odisha, Central Provinces).
  - _Group B:_ Muslim-majority provinces (Punjab, NWFP, Sindh, Balochistan).
  - _Group C:_ Bengal and Assam.
- Central Government to have power over defense, foreign affairs, and communications.
- Provinces to have full autonomy and residual powers.
- Princely States were free to negotiate their status, either to join with Britishers or Successor Governments.
- Constituent Assembly to draft a new constitution; members would be elected by provincial assemblies.
- Interim Government to function till the new constitution and government were formed.
- \[Map: "Cabinet Mission Plan" — coloured map of the subcontinent with Section A (red; Hindu Majority Areas of 1941), Section B (green/teal; Muslim Majority Areas of 1941, north-west), Section C (teal; Bengal & Assam), Princely states not specifically provided for in the proposal (cream); legend: "Current States Boundary", "Current International Boundary"; map labels read "Section A", "Section B", "Section C" and the legend text as noted.\]

**Note:**

- _Attlee's Declaration:_ On 20 February 1947, British Prime Minister Clement Attlee announced in the House of Commons that the British government intended to transfer power to responsible Indian hands before 30 June 1948.

**\[book p.183 / PDF 199\] — right column**

**Response to the Cabinet Mission Plan**

- _Indian National Congress:_ It accepted the plan partially but opposed the idea of grouping, fearing it was a step toward partition.
- _Muslim League:-_ It initially accepted the Cabinet Mission Plan, despite no explicit provision for Pakistan, hoping that the grouping of provinces would eventually lead to a separate state. However, when Muhammad Ali Jinnah realized that grouping was not strictly mandatory, he called for _"Direct Action Day" (16 August 1946)_ demanding Pakistan, this led to horrific communal violence in Calcutta, known as the _"Great Calcutta Killing."_
- _Noakhali Riots_ (Oct-Nov 1946) in Noakhali (now in Bangladesh) began after the Calcutta violence. Mahatma Gandhi visited Noakhali to establish peace.

\[Banner\] **MOUNTBATTEN PLAN**

- Date: 3 June 1947.
- The Mountbatten Plan which formed the basis for Indian Independence Act proposed for the Partition of India into two Dominions namely India and Pakistan. That's why it is also known as _Partition Plan._
- Under this Plan, the Princely States were given the option to _join either India or Pakistan or remain Independent._
  - Out of 565 Princely States, 562 joined either India or Pakistan and 3 did not join initially. They were: J&K, Hyderabad and Junagadh.
- _Lord Louis Mountbatten:_ The last Viceroy of British India and the first Governor-General of independent India (1947–48).
- _C. Rajagopalachari:_ Only Governor-General of independent India (because later this post was removed).
- \[Box\] **Note:** To see Provisions of Indian Independence act 1947 refer to chapter "Important Acts" in Polity section.

\[Banner\] **AFTER INDEPENDENCE**

- _Operation Polo (September 1948):_ Also known as the Hyderabad Police Operation. This operation was led by Major General J.N. Chaudhuri. The Nizam of Hyderabad, Mir Osman Ali Khan, surrendered on 17 September 1948, after which Hyderabad became a part of India.
- _Maharaja Hari Singh_ signed the _"Instrument of Accession"_ on 26 October 1947, officially making Jammu and Kashmir a part of India.
- _Vinayak Damodar Savarkar_ (Hindu Mahasabha): He was a strong supporter of 'Akhand Bharat' (Undivided India) and an opponent of partition. He was not formally a leader of the RSS, but Godse considered him his political guru.

**\[book p.184 / PDF 200\] — left column**

- _Birla House Incident:_ On 30 January 1948, at Birla House in Delhi, during the evening prayer, Nathuram Godse touched Gandhiji's feet and then assassinated him by firing three bullets.
- _Reason for Assassination:_ Godse believed that the partition of the country happened due to Gandhiji's non-violence and Muslim appeasement policy. (Immediate Cause was the release of ₹55 crore to Pakistan by the Indian Government due to Gandhiji's fast).
- _Execution:_ Tried at the Red Fort, _Nathuram Godse_ and his accomplice Narayan Apte were hanged in Ambala Jail on 15 November 1949.
- \[Box\] _Bangladesh Liberation War in 1971 led to the separation of East Pakistan (now Bangladesh) from West Pakistan (now Pakistan). This war took place during the tenure of Indira Gandhi._ / _In 1972, the Shimla Agreement was signed by Indira Gandhi (then Prime Minister of India) and Zulfikar Ali Bhutto (then President of Pakistan) to restore peace between two countries. It has been suspended by Pakistan as a move of retaliation after India suspended Indus Water Treaty_ \[emoji: laughing/tears face\].
- \[Image: film-still photograph of a man in a red turban among a crowd; caption: "Hum Azaad Ho Gaye!"\]
- Mahatma Gandhi was in Noakhali (present day Bangladesh) on _August 15, 1947,_ the day India gained independence.

■■■ \[chapter end marker, red\]

**PART B — Lucent's GK Book (2026): counterpart passages (no separate section — see gandhian_era_raw_extraction.md, Part B)**

Lucent's counterpart to this chapter is the second half of its continuous "19. IV. The Gandhian Era (1917-47)" section, transcribed once in gandhian_era_raw_extraction.md (Part B) and **not re-transcribed here**. Correspondence, in Lucent's order (book p. in brackets):

- Communal Award / Mac Donald Award (Aug. 16, 1932) \[p.79\]; Poona Pact / Gandhi–Ambedkar Pact (Sep. 24, 1932) \[p.79\]; Third Round Table Conference \[p.79\].
- The Government of India Act, 1935; Congress ministries (1937); Congress Ministries Resign (Dec. 22, 1939) \[p.79\].
- Pakistan Resolution / Lahore Resolution (March 24, 1940) \[pp.79–80\].
- August Offer / Linlithgow Offer (Aug. 8, 1940); Individual Civil Disobedience (Oct. 1940–Dec. 1941); The Cripps Mission (March–April 1942); Quit India movement (1942); Gandhiji's Fast (Feb. 10 – March 7, 1943); C.R. Formula (1944); Wavell Plan and Shimla Conference (June 14–July 14, 1945) \[p.80\].
- INA Trial (Nov., 1945); Azad Hind Fauj (INA) box; Royal Indian Navy/Ratings Mutiny (Feb. 18, 1946); Cabinet Mission (March–June, 1946); Direct Action Campaign (Aug. 16, 1946); Interim Government (Sep. 2, 1946); Formation of Constituent Assembly (Dec. 9, 1946); Attlee's Announcement (Feb. 20, 1947); Mountbatten Plan (June 3, 1947); The Indian Independence Act, 1947; Integration of States; French Colonies; Portuguese Colonies \[pp.81–82 top\].
- Gandhi chronology rows 1934-39 … 1948 \[p.77\]; "Note: Gandhi had suggested the winding up of INC…" \[p.77\]. Lucent has **no** counterpart for: Congress Sessions list 1934–39 (Congress Socialist Party, AIKS, NPC, Forward Bloc, Haripura/Tripuri), All India Anti-Untouchability League / Harijan Sevak Sangh (apart from Harijan as journal), Revolutionary Socialist Party, Cripps background factors (Pearl Harbour, Barbarossa), Quit India specific appeals / martyrs / Usha Mehta's underground radio, Desai-Liaqat Pact, Subhash Chandra Bose biography details, Cabinet Mission groups A/B/C, Response to Cabinet Mission, Great Calcutta Killing / Noakhali, Operation Polo, Godse trial/execution, post-1947 boxes.

**C3. Congress sessions 1934–39 (Parmar only in this chapter)**

Lucent has none of these.
