**PART A — Parmar's SSC Fatman (2nd Ed., 2026): "SOCIO RELIGIOUS REFORMS" (book pp.159–161, PDF 175–177)**

Chapter closes with a "■■■" end marker at the bottom of book p.161 (confirmed by direct read). Parmar's chapter map (PDF 170) lists two sub-headings: "Reform Movements in India" and "Socio Religious Reform Movements in South India".

**\[book p.159 / PDF 175\]**

**REFORM MOVEMENTS IN INDIA**

_Reformist Movements started in India due to:_

1. Social evils in society that existed in forms of untouchability, superstition, polygamy (multiple marriages), purdah system, polytheism (worship of more than one God), etc.
2. Position of women deteriorated in society due to polygamy, Sati system and also because widow remarriage was not allowed.

_Steps taken to Ameliorate Women's Position_

_1\. Abolition of Sati_

- _1829:_ An Act passed to abolish _Sati System_ with the efforts of _Raja Ram Mohan Roy_ (_Lord William Bentinck_ was the then Governor-General of India).

_2\. Widow Remarriage_

- _1856: Widow Remarriage Act_ passed with efforts of _Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar_ \[Got this title from his college (Principal in Sanskrit College)\]
- The Act was drafted during the tenure of Lord Dalhousie and enacted in 1856 under Lord Canning.
- Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar was against child marriage.

_Widow Remarriage Association by:_

- _1853: Vishnu Shastri Pandit_ formed _Vidhava Vivaha Uttejaka Mandal._
- _1861: M.G. Ranade_ formed a _Widow Remarriage Association._
- _Satyaprakash_ was a _Gujarati_ language weekly founded by social reformer and journalist _Karsondas Mulji_ with an intention of social reform.

_3\. Child Marriage_

- _1891: Age of Consent Act_ passed due to the efforts of _B. M. Malabari_ (child marriage less than 12 years was not allowed)
- _Sarda Act of 1929:_ It is also known as _Child Marriage Restraint Act_ through which minimum age for marriage at _14 years for girls_ and _18 years for boys_ was set.

_4\. Education_

- _1835: Thomas Babington Macaulay_ presented _Macaulay's Minute_ to promote English education in India.
  - He gave _Downward Filtration Theory._
  - Only rich Indians were to be given education in English medium.
  - _Lord William Bentinck_ (Father of English Education in India) was the Governor-General of India during this time.
- _1854: Wood's Dispatch_
  - It rejected the Downward Filtration Theory.
  - It is also known as _Magna Carta of Indian Education._
  - _(right column)_ — According to Wood's Dispatch Primary level education should be given in Vernacular; Secondary level education in Anglo-Vernacular; Higher level education in English.

_5\. Certain Women's Organisation Formed_

- _Bharat Stree Mahamandal (1910)_ → Founder: _Sarla Devi Chaudhurani._
- _Ladies Social Conference (1904)_ → Founder: _Ramabai Ranade._
- _Women's Indian Association (1917)_ → _Annie Besant, Margaret Cousins_ and _Jeena Raja Dasa._
- _National Council of Women in India (1925)_ → _Lady Meherbai Tata._
- _All India Women's Conference (1927)_ → Founder: _Margaret Cousins._
- _Arya Mahila Samaj (1882)_ → Founder: _Pandita Ramabai Saraswati._

\[Box\] _1st university for Indian Women (SNDT): by Dr Dhondo Keshav Karve (1916)._

_Struggle against Caste based Exploitation (against Chaturvarna System)_

- _Bahishkrit Hitakarini Sabha (1924):_ It was established by _B. R. Ambedkar_ at _Bombay._
- _Self Respect Movement (1925)_ It was started by _E. V. Ramaswamy Naicker_ in _Tamil Nadu._
  - _Same movement started by:_ • Narayan Guru in Kerala. • Jyotiba Phule in Maharashtra.
- _Mahad Satyagraha (1927):_ It was led by _B. R. Ambedkar_ {he also burnt Manusmriti (ancient text upholding caste laws)}.

_Raja Ram Mohan Roy and Brahmo Samaj_

- He is known as _"Father of Indian Renaissance"._
- He was given the title of _"Raja"_ by _Akbar II._
- _1814:_ He established _Atmiya Sabha_ in _Calcutta_ (Forerunner of Brahmo Samaj).
- _1817:_ He founded _Hindu College_ in _Calcutta_ with the help of _David Hare._
- _1825:_ He established _Vedanta College._
- _1828:_ Raja Ram Mohan Roy founded _Brahmo Sabha,_ which later became the _Brahmo Samaj._
- He also translated _Vedas_ in _Bengali._
- He was against idol worship, polytheism, child marriage.

_Writings of Raja Ram Mohan Roy_

- _Gift to Monotheists (Tuhfat-ul-Muwahhidin)._
- _The Precepts of Jesus._
- _Sambad Kaumudi (Bengali)._
- _Mirat-ul-Akhbar (in Persian language)._

\[Box\] _1791: First Hindu College (Banaras) was founded by Jonathan Duncan._

**\[book p.160 / PDF 176\]**

_Debendranath Tagore_

- _1839:_ He started _Tattvabodhini Sabha_ along with its organ _Tattvabodhini Patrika_ in Bengali in Calcutta.
- _1858:_ He invited _Keshav Chandra Sen_ to join the _Brahmo Samaj._
- Due to difference in philosophical thinking, Brahmo Samaj split into two in _1866,_ namely _Brahmo Samaj of India_ (led by _Keshav Chandra Sen_) and _Adi Brahmo Samaj_ (led by _Debendranath Tagore_).
- Later Adi Brahmo Samaj also got divided and _Sadharan Brahmo Samaj_ was founded by _Ananda Mohan Bose_ in _1878._

_Veda Samaj_

- _1864:_ Veda Samaj was formed in _Madras_ by _Keshav Chandra Sen_ and _K. Sridharalu Naidu._
- It is also known as _Brahmo Samaj of South India._

_Young Bengal Movement (Late 1820s and Early 1830s)_

- Started by: _Henry Vivian Derozio._
- He was a teacher at the Hindu College.

_Dharma Sabha → Revivalist Movement (1830)_

- It was started by _Radhakanta Deb_ → He was against the idea of Brahmo Samaj.

\*Paramhansa Mandali (1849) → (\*_SSC considers this year as 1840)_

- It was formed by _Dadoba Pandurang_ and _Durgaram Mehtaji_ in _Maharashtra._

_Prarthana Samaj (1867)_

- It was formed by _Atmaram Pandurang_ in _Maharashtra._
- _M.G. Ranade_ joined it in _1870, R G Bhandarkar_ was also a member.

_Deoband Movement (1866)_

- Started by: _Muhammad Qasim Nanautavi_ and _Rashid Ahmad Gangohi._
- _Launched in:_ Saharanpur district in Uttar Pradesh.

_Singh Sabha Movement (1873)_

- It started in _Amritsar._
- It aimed to revive Sikh faith by bringing back the converts.

_Satyasodhak Samaj (1873)_

- It was formed by _Jyotiba Phule_ (he was from Mali/Gardener community) in _Maharashtra_ against caste discrimination.
- In _1848,_ Jyotiba Phule along with his wife _Savitri Phule_ (first female teacher of India) started the first school for girls in _Pune._
- Books written by Jyotiba Phule: _Gulamgiri, Sarvajanik Satyadharma_ (both in Marathi).

_Arya Samaj (1875)_

- It was founded by _Swami Dayanand Saraswati_ (original name: _Mool Shankar_).
- First unit of Arya Samaj was opened at _Bombay_ and later shifted to _Lahore._
- He advocated for _Caste less_ and _Class less society._
- He gave slogans _"Go Back to Vedas"_ and _"India for Indians"._
- He considered Vedas as infallible but criticised Puranas.
- He believed in Karma and Reincarnation.
- He subscribed (support) the Chaturvarna system on the basis of work not by birth.
- He was against the Idol Worship.
- Book written by him: _Satyarth Prakash._
- _1886:_ Dayanand Anglo-Vedic (DAV) College in Lahore was established in memory of Swami Dayanand by _Mahatma Hansraj._
- _1893:_ The Arya Samaj members in Punjab were divided on the question of vegetarianism and modern education. The group that refrained from eating meat were called the _"Mahatma"_ group and the other group, the _"Cultured Party"._

_Aligarh Movement (1875)_

- Started by: _Sir Syed Ahmed Khan._
- He established a _Mohammaden Anglo Oriental College_ in 1875 which was later called _Aligarh Muslim University_ in 1920.
- He also founded _Indian Patriotic Association_ in 1888.
- _Magazine: Tahdhib-ul-Akhlaq_ (improvement of manners and morals).

_Dev Samaj (1887)_

- Founder: _Shiv Narayan Agnihotri_ at _Lahore._

_Shuddhi Movement_

- It was a purification movement initiated by the _Arya Samaj_ to reconvert people who had converted to other religions (especially Islam and Christianity) back to Hinduism.

_Swami Vivekananda_

- He started the _Ramkrishna Movement._
- _Original Name: Narendranath Dutta._
- His birth: _12 Jan 1863_ is celebrated as _"National Youth Day"._
- Died on: _4 July 1902._
- He believed in idol worship.
- Books written: _Raja Yoga, Karma Yoga._
- _1893:_ Swami Vivekananda gave a speech in _Chicago (USA)_ in a conference.
- He advocated on how to make balance between Materialism and Spiritualism.
- _Swami Vivekananda Rock Memorial_ is in _Tamil Nadu (Kanyakumari)._
- \[Image: portrait of Swami Vivekananda\]

**\[book p.161 / PDF 177\]**

_Ramkrishna Mission/Ramkrishna Movement (1897)_

- It was started by _Swami Vivekananda_ in the memory of his _Guru Ramkrishna Paramhansa_ (Priest of Kali Mandir in Calcutta) → _Original name: Gadadhar Chattopadhyay._
- HQ: _Belur Math; Howrah (West Bengal)._
- According to him _"Service to man is service to God"._

_Balshashtri Jambhekar_

- He started two newspapers: _Darpan_ and _Digdarshan._
- He is remembered as the _"Father of Marathi Journalism"._

_Gopal Hari Deshmukh_

- Pen name: _Lokhitwadi._
- He started weekly newspapers: _Prabhakar_ and _Hitechhu._

_Gopal Ganesh Agarkar_

- He started a weekly: _Sudharak._

_Servants of India Society (1905)_

- Founder: _Gopal Krishna Gokhale._
- He was political Guru of _Gandhi_ and _Jinnah._
- To promote education and social service in India.

_Seva Sadan (1908)_

- Founder: _B. M. Malabari._
- He worked against child marriage and for widow remarriage.

_Social Service League (1911)_

- Founder: _Narayan Malhar Joshi_ at _Bombay._
- He also started _All India Trade Union Congress_ in 1920.

_Wahabi Movement_

- \[Box\] _Faraizi Movement (1818)_ • Started by: _Haji Shariatullah._
- Started by: _Sayyid Ahmad Barelvi_ of Rae Bareli.
- It was a Muslim revivalist movement, popularly known as _Tariqa-e-Muhammadiya._
- Syed Ahmad Barelvi and his followers worked to transform India from _Dar-ul-Harb_ (land of non-Islamic rule) into _Dar-ul-Islam_ (land governed by Islamic principles).

**SOCIO RELIGIOUS REFORM MOVEMENTS IN SOUTH INDIA** _(sub-heading as printed, mid-left-column of book p.161; everything below it to the chapter end is under this heading in the printed layout, including entries that are not South Indian, e.g. Satnami, Theosophical Society, Indian National Social Conference)_

_Satnami Movement_

- It started in 1820s in _Chhatisgarh_ region by _Guru Ghasidas_ who belonged to Satnami sect.
- He worked with the leatherworkers (around 1820) to improve their social status.

_Theosophical Society (1875)_

- It was started in _New York (USA)_ by _HP Blavatsky_ and _HS Olcott._
- The society's HQ was shifted to _Adyar Madras_ from New York in _1882._
- Later _Annie Besant_ took its membership.
- This society was highly influenced by Hindu belief system.

_Indian National Social Conference (1887)_

- Founders: _M. G. Ranade_ and _Raghunath Rao._
- _Pledge Movement_ was started by them against child marriage.

_Shree Narayana Guru Dharam Paripalana (SNDP) Movement_

- Started by: _Shree Narayan Guru Swami_ for the upliftment of _Ezhavas Community_ of Kerala.
- Important slogan: _"Oru Jathi, Oru Matham, Oru Daivam"_ (One Caste, One Religion, One God for All).

_Justice Movement (1917)_

- Started by: _C. N. Mudaliar, T. M. Nair_ and _P. Tyagaraja._

_Self Respect Movement (1925)_

- Founder: _E.V. Ramaswamy Naicker_ (also known as _Periyar_) in Tamil Nadu.
- Objective: Rejection of Brahminical dominance and rituals.

\[Box\] _Note : The Civil Disobedience Movement in Kerala was led by K. Kelappan._ / _Amar Jiban by Rassundari Devi → 1st known autobiography written by an Indian Woman._

_Temple Entry Movement (for the entry of Dalits in temple)_

- Temple entry movement was started by _Dr B.R. Ambedkar_ in _1927._
- In _South India (1924),_ it was started by _T. K. Madhavan._
- _Vaikom Satyagraha (1924),_ it was started by _K.P. Kesava Menon._

\[Box\] _Stri Purush Tulna was written by: Tarabai Shinde._

\[Image: Kalaram Temple, Nashik — caption: "B. R. Ambedkar started Temple Entry Movement in 1930 here for the entry of Dalits in this temple"\]

\[Box\] • _All India Depressed Classes Association (1926):_ Formed in Nagpur, led by _M.C. Rajah._ • _All India Depressed Classes Congress (1930):_ Led by _Dr. B.R. Ambedkar._

**Important Socio-Religious Reformers**

_Swami Sahajanand (1781-1830)_ : His original name being Gyanashyama, founded the _Swaminarayan sect_ in Gujarat, which believed in a theistic God and prescribed a moral code for its followers.

_Raja Rammohan Roy (1772-1833)_ : Born in 1772 at Radhanagar in Hoogly district (West Bengal) founded _Atmiya Sabha_ in Calcutta in 1815 to propagate monotheism and reforms in the Hindu society. The Atmiya Sabha was named _Brahmo Sabha_ and finally _Brahmo Samaj_ in 1828. Launched a movement for the abolition of Sati through his journal _Sabad Kaumudi_ (1819).

_Debendranath Tagore (1817-1905)_ : Took over the leadership of the Brahmo Samaj after Raja Rammohan Roy. Founded _Tattvabodhini Sabha_ in 1839 and published _Tattvabodhini Patrika_, a Bengali monthly to propagate the ideas of Raja Rammohan Roy. In 1859, the Tattvabodhini Sabha was amalgamated with the Brahmo Samaj. He compiled selected passages from the Upanishads, which came to be known as _Brahma Dharma_.

**\[book p.72 / PDF 82, left column\]**

_Keshav Chandra Sen (1838-1884)_ : Keshav Chandra Sen was the leader of the Brahmo Samaj during the absence of Debendranath Tagore. He started _\[title partly obscured by a scan blemish — legible only as "…abodhini Patrika"\]_, a journal for women. He launched radical reforms, such as giving up of caste names, inter-caste and widow remarriages and launched movement against child marriages. These radical reforms led to the first schism in the Brahmo Samaj. The original Brahmo Samaj came to be known as _Adi Brahmo Samaj_ and the other, the _Brahmo Samaj of India_ which was established by Keshav Chandra Sen in 1866. Sen formed the _\[partly obscured: "…ndian Reform Association"\]_ in 1870, which persuaded the British Government to enact the _Native Marriage Act of 1872 (popularly known as Civil Marriage Act)_ legalising the Brahmo marriages and fixing the minimum marriageable age for boys and girls.

_Atmaram Pandurang (1823-1898)_ : Atmaram Pandurang founded _Prarthana Samaj_ in 1867 in Bombay. M.G. Ranade joined it in 1870.

_Swami Dayanand Saraswati (1824-1883)_ : Swami Dayanand Saraswati, originally known as _Mula Shankar_ founded the _Arya Samaj_ in 1875 in Bombay, wrote _Satyartha Prakash (in Hindi)_ and _Veda-Bhashya Bhumika (partly in Hindi and partly in Sanskrit)_.

_Blavatsky (1831-91) and Olcott (1832-1907)_ : Madam H.P. Blavatsky, a Russian woman and Col. H.S. Olcott, an American, founded the _Theosophical Society_ in New York in 1875, but shifted the headquarter of the Society to Adyar near Madras in 1882.

_Swami Vivekanand (1863-1902)_ : Swami Vivekanand _(originally Narendranath Dutta)_, founded the _Ramakrishna Mission_ in 1897 as a social service league which was registered as a Trust in 1897.

**PART C — Cross-book notes / discrepancies (books only — no test cross-referencing)**

**C1. Where each book's material sits**

- Parmar: one 3-page chapter (book pp.159–161), organised by _reformer/movement_ with short bullet blocks, plus themed blocks (women, education, caste struggle).
- Lucent: ch.18 (book p.71 right–p.72 left) — organised as (1) a year-wise table of 25 organisations, (2) short reformer biographies for 8 figures, (3) a 9-row lower-caste/caste-movement table. Lucent has **no** women's-organisation list, **no** education-policy material (Macaulay/Wood's Dispatch), **no** Widow-Remarriage/Sati/Sarda-Act legislation, **no** Wahabi/Faraizi/Satnami/Young Bengal/Veda Samaj/Singh Sabha/Aligarh-College/DAV/Shuddhi/Kelappan/Temple-Entry material. Parmar has **no** equivalent to Lucent's Swami Sahajanand (Swaminarayan sect), Nirankari/Namdhari/Radha Swami/Manav Dharma Sabha/Nadwah-ul-Ulama/Muhammadan Educational Conference/Deccan Education Society/Poona Seva Sadan/Seva Samiti/Harijan Sevak Sangh/Depressed Class Mission/Bahujan Samaj/Aravippuram entries.

**C3. Points to re-check against a fresh page render before relying on them**

- The Deoband founder(s) conflict (Lucent's "Maulana Hussain Ahmed" vs Parmar's "Muhammad Qasim Nanautavi and Rashid Ahmad Gangohi") — a genuine cross-book disagreement; no third source consulted (I do not have a verified source for the correct historical founders beyond what Parmar prints — verify externally if this matters).
- Keshav Chandra Sen's women's journal title in Lucent (obscured by a scan blemish) — Parmar's chapter does not name it either, so it cannot be filled from either book here.

**C4. Overlap with existing project extractions**

- **Bhakti/Sufi Movements** (medieval, claude/medieval-history-raw/bhakti_sufi_movements_raw_extraction.md) — no overlap: that file covers medieval bhakti/Sufi saints and Sikh Gurus; this file is 19th–20th-century reform movements. The Singh Sabha Movement (1873) here is the only Sikh-reform item; Guru Gobind Singh's Punjab-history line appears in Lucent's ch.16 (see the Advent/Expansion file), not here.
- **Ancient/Medieval raw files**: no overlap.
- **Freedom-struggle chapters (Modern History, this task)**: the Ilbert-era/press/education items that Lucent covers elsewhere (if any) will be cross-referenced in those chapter files, not repeated here.
